{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
is abelian, we may replace
h
by
hy
to conclude that
h^{-1}n\sigma(h)\in A_{0}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{0^{+}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.5
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame and
n\bar{\sigma}
has order
\ell
in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
. If
\xi
is a primitive
\ell^{\text{th}}
root of unity in
K
, then

n
is
\sigma
-conjugate by an element of
\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
to an element of
A_{0}
of the form
\lambda(\xi)
where
\lambda\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\tilde{K}
is a tame extension of
K
.

Let
\xi
be a primitive
\ell^{\text{th}}
root of unity in
K
and let
\bar{\xi}
denote the image of
\xi
in
\mathfrak{F}
.

From Lemma
3.2.4
we can choose
h\in\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
such that
h^{-1}n\sigma(h)\in A_{0}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{0^{+}}
.

Choose a sequence
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<\cdots
such that for all
s>0
we have
\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s}\neq\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s^{+}}
if and only if
s=r_{j}
for some
j
. Here
\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s}
and
\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s^{+}}
are Moy-Prasad subgroups
[
32
,
33
]
of
\tilde{G}
. For
s>0
set
{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s}={\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}\cap\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s}
,
{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s^{+}}={\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}\cap\tilde{G}_{x_{0},s^{+}}
and
{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s:s^{+}}={\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s}/{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s^{+}}
.
Similarly, define

,
{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{s^{+}}={A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}\cap G_{x_{0},s^{+}}
, and

.

For every
j\geq 1

the linear map
\bar{y}\mapsto(1-\bar{\sigma})\bar{y}
from the
\mathfrak{F}
-vector space

{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}:r_{j}^{+}}/{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}:r_{j}^{+}}
to itself is an isomorphism. Thus
for every element
x\in{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}}
there exists
{y}\in{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}}
such that
\bar{\sigma}(y)^{-1}xy\in{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}^{+}}
.

Applying this to
h^{-1}n\sigma(h)\in A_{0}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{0^{+}}=A_{0}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{1}}
, we conclude, using the fact that
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
is abelian, that there exists
y_{1}\in{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{1}}
such that if
h_{1}=hy_{1}
we have
h_{1}^{-1}n\sigma(h_{1})\in A_{0}{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{0^{+}}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{2}}
. We may repeat this process to conclude that there exists
y_{j}\in{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j}}
such that if
h_{j}=(h_{j-1})y_{j}
, then
h_{j}^{-1}n\sigma(h_{j})\in A_{0}{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{0^{+}}{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}_{r_{j+1}}
.
Letting
j
go to infinity we conclude that there is an
h_{\infty}\in\mathbf{G}(\tilde{L})_{x_{0},0}
such that
h_{\infty}^{-1}n\sigma(h_{\infty})\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(L)_{0}=A_{0}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(L)_{0^{+}}
.
Here
L
denotes the completion of
K
and
\tilde{L}=L\otimes_{K}\tilde{K}
denotes the completion of
\tilde{K}
; note that
\bar{\sigma}
extends (continuously) to an automorphism of
\tilde{L}
whose fixed point set is

.

Choose
a\in A_{0}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(L)_{0^{+}}

such that
h_{\infty}^{-1}n\sigma(h_{\infty})=a
.
Since the order of
n\bar{\sigma}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
is
\ell
, we have

N_{\ell}^{\bar{\sigma}}(n)=n\bar{\sigma}(n)\bar{\sigma}^{2}(n)\cdots\bar{\sigma}^{\ell-2}(n)\bar{\sigma}^{\ell-1}(n)=1.

Thus, since
a=\sigma^{j}(a)
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
\sigma^{\ell}(h_{\infty})=h_{\infty}
, we have

a^{\ell}=\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\sigma^{j}(a)=\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\sigma^{j}(h_{\infty}^{-1}n\sigma(h_{\infty}))=h_{\infty}^{-1}N_{\ell}^{\bar{\sigma}}(n)\sigma^{\ell}(h_{\infty})=1.

Let
\bar{a}
denote the image of
a
in
\mathsf{A}
.
Since
\bar{a}^{\ell}=1
, there exists
\lambda\in\X_{*}(\mathsf{A})
such that
\bar{a}=\lambda(\bar{\xi})
. Since
\X_{*}(\mathsf{A})=\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
, we may and do think of
\lambda
as an element of
\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
. Thus,
a=\lambda(\xi)\tilde{a}
where
\tilde{a}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(L)_{0^{+}}
. Since
1=a^{\ell}=\tilde{a}^{\ell}
and
\ell
is coprime to
p
, we conclude that
\tilde{a}=1
. Thus,
a=\lambda(\xi)\in A_{0}
.

If
k\neq K
, in which case
L
may not be
K
, we
need to show that we can take
h_{\infty}\in\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
. We proceed as follows.
Choose finite extensions
k^{\prime}\leq\tilde{k}^{\prime}
of
k
with
k^{\prime}\leq K
and
\tilde{k}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{K}
such that
\Gal(\tilde{k}^{\prime}/k^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
\Gal(\tilde{K}/K)
,
\mathbf{A}
is a maximal
k^{\prime}
split torus in
\mathbf{G}
,
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
is a maximal
\tilde{k}^{\prime}
-split torus in
\mathbf{G}
,

x_{0}\in{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbf{G},k^{\prime})
, we have
h,n\in\mathbf{G}(\tilde{k}^{\prime})_{x_{0},0}
,
\xi\in k^{\prime}
, etc. The entire proof above goes through with
{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
replaced by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(\tilde{k}^{\prime})
,
{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
replaced by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(k^{\prime})
, etc. Since
k^{\prime}
is complete, we conclude that we have
h_{\infty}\in\mathbf{G}(\tilde{k}^{\prime})_{x_{0},0}\leq\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
.
∎

By taking the reductive quotient, we recover a very special version of the known general result that if
\bar{g}\in\mathsf{G}
such that
\bar{g}\sigma
has finite order coprime to
p
, then
g
is
\sigma
-conjugate in
\mathsf{G}
to an element of
\mathsf{A}
:

Corollary 3.2.6
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame and
n\bar{\sigma}
has order
\ell
in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
. Let
\bar{n}\in N_{\mathsf{G}}(\mathsf{A})
denote the image of
n
in
\mathsf{G}
. If
\xi
is a primitive
\ell^{\text{th}}
root of unity in
\mathfrak{F}
, then

\bar{n}
is
\sigma
-conjugate by an element of
\mathsf{G}
to an element of
\mathsf{A}
of the form
\lambda(\xi)
where
\lambda\in\X_{*}(\mathsf{A})
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
3.2.5
by looking at the reductive quotient.
∎

Remark 3.2.7
.

Note that
the element
\lambda\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})=\X_{*}(\mathsf{A})
encodes what is known as the
Kac coordinates
or, equivalently, the
Kac diagram
of the image of
\bar{n}\sigma
in
\Aut(\Lie(\mathsf{G}))
. Note that since
\mu(\xi)=1
for all
\mu\in\ell\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
, the element
\lambda
cannot be unique. First introduced by Kac
in
[
21
]
(see also
[
20
, Chapter X, Section 5]
or
[
22
, Chapter 8]
), the use of Kac coordinates for
p
-adic groups was initiated in
[
40
]
and
[
41
]
. We will have more to say about Kac coordinates in Section
3.5
.

3.3.
From tame elements of
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
to maximal
K
-tori in
\mathbf{G}

Mark Reeder is responsible for the key ideas of this section: the definition of the element
g_{n}
and how to use
g_{n}
to define the maximal
K
-torus
\mathbf{T}_{n}
of
\mathbf{G}
. I thank him for explaining these ideas to me; I am responsible for all errors.

Choose a tame
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
, and suppose
n\bar{\sigma}
has order
\ell_{n}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
.

Recall that
\varpi\in k
is a uniformizer. Choose a uniformizer
\pi\in K_{n}
such that
\pi^{\ell_{n}}=\varpi
. Set
\xi=\sigma(\pi)/\pi
; note that
\xi\in K
is a primitive
\ell_{n}^{\text{th}}
root of unity.
Thanks to Lemma
3.2.5

there exist
h\in\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
and
\lambda_{n}\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
such that
h^{-1}n\sigma(h)=\lambda_{n}(\xi)
. Define
x_{n}\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(A)
by
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}
, define
g_{n}\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}
by
g_{n}=\lambda_{n}(\pi)h^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\pi)^{-1}
, and define the maximal
K_{n}
-split torus
\mathbf{T}_{n}
of
\mathbf{G}
by
\mathbf{T}={{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
.

Remark 3.3.1
.

The point
x_{n}=\lambda_{n}(\pi)\cdot x_{0}
is an absolutely special vertex in
{\mathcal{B}}^{\red}(\mathbf{G},K_{n})
.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

The torus
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is defined over
K
.

Proof.

We first compute
g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})
:

\begin{split}g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})&=(\lambda_{n}(\pi)h\lambda_{n}(\pi)^{-1})(\sigma(\lambda_{n}(\pi)h^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\pi)^{-1}))=\lambda_{n}(\pi)h\lambda_{n}(\pi)^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\xi)\lambda_{n}(\pi)\sigma(h)^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\pi\xi)^{-1}\\
&=\lambda_{n}(\pi)h\lambda_{n}(\xi)\sigma(h)^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\pi\xi)^{-1}=\lambda_{n}(\pi)n\lambda_{n}(\pi\xi)^{-1}=n[n^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\pi)n\lambda_{n}(\pi\xi)]\\
&=na^{\prime}\end{split}

where
a^{\prime}={{{}^{n^{-1}}\!}\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\lambda_{n}(\pi\xi)\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
. Consequently,
\sigma(\mathbf{T}_{n})=\sigma({{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})={{}^{g_{n}na^{\prime}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=\mathbf{T}_{n}
, and so
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is defined over
K
.
∎

While the maximal
K
-torus
\mathbf{T}_{n}
of
\mathbf{G}
depends on many choices, we now show that the
G
-conjugacy class of
\mathbf{T}_{n}
depends only on the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of the image of
n
in
\tilde{W}
.

Suppose
w\in\tilde{W}
. If
w^{\prime}
is
\sigma
-conjugate to
w
, then
w^{\prime}
and
w
have the same order in
\tilde{W}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
. Thus, if
w
is tame, then every element in the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w
is tame as well. Let
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
denote the set of tame elements in
\tilde{W}
. Let
W_{\sim_{\sigma}}
(resp.
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}
) denote the set of
\sigma
-conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}
(resp.
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
).

Definition 3.3.3
.

We let
\mathcal{C}
denote the set of
G
-conjugacy classes of maximal
K
-tori in
\mathbf{G}
.

Corollary 3.3.4
.

The assignment
n\mapsto T_{n}
from tame elements in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
to maximal
K
-tori in
\mathbf{G}
induces an injective map

\varphi_{\sigma}\colon\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}.

Proof.

For
w_{i}\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
with
i\in\{1,2\}
we let
n_{i}\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
be in the preimage of
w_{i}
under the natural projection
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\rightarrow\tilde{W}
. Recall that to the element
n_{i}
we can associate
g_{i}\in\mathbf{G}(k_{\dtame})
such that
g_{i}^{-1}\sigma(g_{i}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=n_{i}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
denote the
G
-orbit of
\mathbf{T}_{n_{i}}={{}^{g_{i}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
.

We first show that the orbit
\mathcal{O}_{i}
depends only on the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w_{i}
.
Suppose
{\mathbf{T}}\in\mathcal{O}_{i}
. Then
\mathbf{T}
is
k_{\dtame}
-split and so there exists
g\in\mathbf{G}(k_{\dtame})
such that
{\mathbf{T}}={{}^{{g}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Let
{w}^{\prime}
denote the image of
g^{-1}\sigma(g)\in N_{\mathbf{G}(k_{\dtame})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
in
\tilde{W}
. Since
{\mathbf{T}}
is
G
-conjugate to
\mathbf{T}_{i}
, there exists
k_{i}\in G
such that
{\mathbf{T}}={{}^{k_{i}}\!}{\mathbf{T}_{i}}
. Thus
{{}^{g}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={{}^{k_{i}{g_{i}}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Consequently, there exists
n\in N_{\mathbf{G}(k_{\dtame})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
g_{i}^{-1}k_{i}^{-1}{g}=n
, so
k_{i}^{-1}{g}=g_{i}n
. Note that

w^{\prime}=g^{-1}\sigma(g){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=(k_{i}^{-1}g)^{-1}\sigma(k_{i}^{-1}g){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=(g_{i}n)^{-1}\sigma(g_{i}n){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={n}^{-1}n_{i}\sigma(n){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=u^{-1}w_{i}\sigma(u)

where
u
denotes the image of
n
in
\tilde{W}
.
Thus,
w^{\prime}
is
\sigma
-conjugate to
w_{i}
.

We now show that if
w_{1}
is
\sigma
-conjugate to
w_{2}
, then
\mathcal{O}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{2}
. If
w_{1}
is
\sigma
-conjugate to
w_{2}
, then there exists
n\in N_{\tilde{G}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
n^{-1}n_{2}\sigma(n){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=n_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
, or

(g_{2}n)^{-1}\sigma(g_{2}n){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=g_{1}^{-1}\sigma(g_{1}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Consequently, there exists
t\in\mathbf{T}_{1}(k_{\dtame})
such that

g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1}t=\sigma(g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1})
. Thus, for all
\gamma\in T_{1}
, we have

\sigma({{}^{g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1}}\!}\gamma)={{}^{\sigma(g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1})}\!}\gamma={{}^{g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1}t}\!}\gamma={{}^{g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1}}\!}\gamma.

That is,
g_{2}ng_{1}^{-1}
maps
K
-points of
T_{1}
to
K
-points of
T_{2}
. Thus, by Lemma
2.1.1
, we have that
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are
G
-conjugate. That is,
\mathcal{O}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{2}
. We can therefore define a function
\varphi_{\sigma}\colon\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
by sending the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w_{i}\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
to the
G
-conjugacy class of
\mathbf{T}_{i}
.

Finally, we show that
\varphi_{\sigma}
is injective. Suppose
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are two
\sigma
-conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
with
{\varphi_{\sigma}}(c_{1})={\varphi_{\sigma}}(c_{2})
.
Suppose
w_{i}\in c_{i}
. Since
{\varphi_{\sigma}}(c_{1})={\varphi_{\sigma}}(c_{2})
, there exists
g\in G
such that
{{}^{g}\!}\mathbf{T}_{1}=\mathbf{T}_{2}
, and so
{{}^{gg_{1}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={{}^{g_{2}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Consequently, there exists
n^{\prime}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(k_{\dtame})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
g_{1}^{-1}g^{-1}{g_{2}}=n^{\prime}
, so
g^{-1}{g_{2}}=g_{1}n^{\prime}
. Note that

n_{2}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=g_{2}^{-1}\sigma(g_{2}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=(g^{-1}g_{2})^{-1}\sigma(g^{-1}g_{2}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=(g_{1}n^{\prime})^{-1}\sigma(g_{1}n^{\prime}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={n^{\prime}}^{-1}n_{1}\sigma(n^{\prime}){\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}.

Consequently,
c_{1}=c_{2}
.
∎

Definition 3.3.5
.

An element
w\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
is called
\sigma
-elliptic provided that the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w
does not intersect a proper parabolic subgroup
\tilde{W}_{\theta}
of
\tilde{W}
where
\theta\subset\tilde{\Pi}
,
\theta\neq\tilde{\Pi}
, and
\sigma(\theta)=\theta
. Here
\tilde{W}_{\theta}
is the subgroup of
\tilde{W}
generated by the simple reflections corresponding to the roots in
\theta
. An element
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
will be called
\sigma
-elliptic
provided that its image in
\tilde{W}
is

\sigma
-elliptic. A
\sigma
-conjugacy class in
\tilde{W}
is called
\sigma
-elliptic provided that some (hence any) element in
c
is
\sigma
-elliptic.

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame. We have

n
is
\sigma
-elliptic if and only if
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is
K
-minisotropic. Moreover, if
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is
K
-minisotropic, then
x_{T_{n}}=x_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\bar{n}
denote the image of
n
in
\tilde{W}
.

Suppose
n
is not
\sigma
-elliptic. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\bar{n}\in\tilde{W}_{\theta}
for some proper
\theta\subset\tilde{\Pi}
with
\sigma(\theta)=\theta
. Let
\mathbf{M}_{\theta}
denote the Levi subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
that contains
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
and corresponds to
\theta
. That is,
\mathbf{M}_{\theta}
is the centralizer of
(\cap_{\alpha\in\theta}\ker(\alpha))^{\circ}
. Note that
(\bar{n},\bar{\sigma})
is tame in
\tilde{W}_{\theta}\rtimes\tilde{\Gamma}
and we can construct
g_{n}
in
\mathbf{M}_{\theta}(k_{\dtame})
for which
g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})
has image
\bar{n}
in
\tilde{W}_{\theta}
. By construction,
{{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}\leq\mathbf{M}_{\theta}
is a
K
-torus which is not
K
-minisotropic for
\mathbf{G}
.

Suppose
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is not
K
-minisotropic. After conjugating by an element of
G
, we may assume that
\mathbf{T}_{n}\subset\mathbf{M}_{\theta}
for some
\sigma
-stable proper
\theta\subset\tilde{\Pi}
. Since
{{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
and
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
are
k_{\dtame}
-split tori in
\mathbf{M}_{\theta}
, there exists
m\in\mathbf{M}_{\theta}(k_{\dtame})
such that
{{}^{g_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={{}^{m}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. As in the proof of Corollary
3.3.4
we have that the images of
m^{-1}\sigma(m)
and
g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})
in
\tilde{W}
are
\sigma
-conjugate. Since the image of
m^{-1}\sigma(m)
belongs to
\tilde{W}_{\theta}
, we conclude that
n
is not
\sigma
-elliptic.

Suppose
\mathbf{T}_{n}
is
K
-minisotropic. Since
T_{n}
splits over the tame extension
K_{n}
, the point
x_{n}
is the
\sigma
-fixed point of the image of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(\mathbf{T}_{n},K_{n})
in
{\mathcal{B}}^{\red}(\mathbf{G},K_{n})
. Hence, we have
x_{n}=x_{T_{n}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.7
.

w\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
is
\sigma
-elliptic if and only if the only fixed point for the action of
w\sigma
on
\X_{*}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})/\X_{*}(\mathbf{Z})
is
0
.

Proof.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is a lift of
w
.
Since the action of
\sigma
on
\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{T},K_{n})
corresponds to the action of
w\sigma
on
\mathcal{A}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}},K_{n})
, the result follows from Lemma
3.3.6
.
∎

Remark 3.3.8
.

Suppose
w\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
is
\sigma
-elliptic. Since any two lifts of
w
into
N_{\tilde{G}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
are
{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
-conjugate, we conclude that any two lifts of
w
into
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\leq\tilde{G}^{\prime}
have the same order. Thus, when
w\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
is
\sigma
-elliptic, the
\ell
and
K_{n}
of Definition
3.1.4
depend only on the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w
.

Lemma 3.3.9
.

If
w\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
is
\sigma
-elliptic, then we may choose
g_{n}
in Lemma
3.3.2
such that
g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})=z\cdot{{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}n
for some
z\in\tilde{Z}_{0}
.

Remark 3.3.10
.

Note that
g_{n}^{-1}\sigma(g_{n})\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}
. Also, since
\tilde{W}\leq\tilde{G}^{\prime}
, we can replace
\mathbf{G}
by
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
in every result of Section
3.3
to this point. Thus, we can choose
h,g_{n}\in\tilde{G}^{\prime}
, hence
z\in\tilde{Z}_{0}\cap\tilde{G}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\mathsf{Z}}
denote the maximal split torus in the center of
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}
; note that this is the image of
\tilde{Z}_{0}\cap\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}
in
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}
.
Since
w
is
\sigma
-elliptic, the map
1-w\sigma\colon\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}/\tilde{\mathsf{Z}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}/\tilde{\mathsf{Z}}
is surjective. Thus, by using an approximation argument as in the proof of Lemma
3.2.5
, we can produce
a\in{\tilde{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
and
z\in\tilde{Z}_{0}
such that
an\sigma(a)^{-1}n^{-1}=z\cdot{{}^{n}\!}\lambda_{n}(\xi)
. In the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
replace
h
, which has the property that
h\lambda_{n}(\xi)\sigma(h)^{-1}=n
,
with
\tilde{h}:=ah
and replace
g_{n}
with
\tilde{g}:={{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}\tilde{h}^{-1}
to obtain

\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}\sigma(\tilde{g}_{n})={{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}(ah\lambda_{n}(\xi)\sigma(h)^{-1}\sigma(a)^{-1}\lambda_{n}(\xi)^{-1})={{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}(an\sigma(a)^{-1}n^{-1}n\lambda_{n}(\xi)^{-1})=z\cdot{{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}n.

Note that
{{}^{\tilde{g}_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}={{}^{{g}_{n}}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
.
∎

As in
[
41
, Theorem 4.1]
is also possible to explicitly describe the reductive quotient at the point
x_{n}
:

Lemma 3.3.11
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame. The map
\Ad(\lambda_{n}(\pi))\colon\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}

identifies
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
with
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}^{\lambda_{n}(\xi)}
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}={\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}
and
\lambda_{n}(\xi)
centralizes
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
,
it suffices to look at what happens on root subgroups of
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}
.

Suppose
\alpha\in\Phi(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{A})
. Choose
\dot{\alpha}\in\Phi(\mathbf{G},{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
satisfying
\res_{\mathbf{A}}(\dot{\alpha})=\alpha
. Since
\lambda_{n}\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
, we have
\langle\lambda_{n},\alpha\rangle=\langle\lambda_{n},\dot{\alpha}\rangle=\langle\lambda_{n},\sigma\dot{\alpha}\rangle
where
\langle\,,\,\rangle\colon\X_{*}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})\times\X^{*}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
denotes the natural pairing. For all
X
in the root space
{\mathfrak{g}}(K_{n})_{\dot{\alpha}}
, we have
\sigma({{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}X)=\xi^{\langle\lambda_{n},\alpha\rangle}\cdot{{}^{\lambda_{n}(\pi)}\!}(\sigma(X))
. Thus, if
U_{\alpha}
denotes the unipotent subgroup of
G
corresponding to
\alpha
, then
\Ad(\lambda_{n}(\pi))
maps
U_{\alpha}\cap G_{x_{0},0}
to
U_{\alpha}\cap G_{x_{n},0}
if and only if
\xi^{\langle\lambda_{n},\alpha\rangle}=1
. Since
x_{0}
is absolutely special,
the result follows.
∎

Lemma 3.3.12
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame.
We have
G_{x_{0},0}^{\lambda_{n}}\leq G_{x_{n},0}
.

Proof.

If
k\in G_{x_{0},0}^{\lambda_{n}}
, then
k\in G_{x_{0}+r\lambda_{n},0}
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Thus
G_{x_{0},0}^{\lambda_{n}}\leq G_{x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n},0}=G_{x_{n},0}
.
∎

When
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, we revisit and sharpen this result in Section
3.5
.

3.4.
Parameterizing maximal tame
K
-tori of
\mathbf{G}

Recall that a
K
-torus
\mathbf{T}
of
\mathbf{G}
will be called
\emph{tame}
provided that
\mathbf{T}
is
k_{\dtame}
-split. Note that if
\mathbf{T}
is tame, then every
G
-conjugate of
\mathbf{T}
is tame as well. We let
\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
denote the set of
G
-conjugacy classes of tame maximal
K
-tori of
\mathbf{G}
.

Lemma 3.4.1
.

The image of the injective map

\varphi_{\sigma}\colon\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}

of Corollary
3.3.4

is
\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
.

Proof.

By construction, the image of
\varphi_{\sigma}
lies in
\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
. So, we only need to show that if
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
, then there exists
c\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}
such that
\varphi_{\sigma}(c)=\mathcal{O}
. Fix
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
. Choose
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}
. Let
E\leq k_{\dtame}
be the Galois extension over which
\mathbf{T}
splits. From Lemma
1.4.2
we have that
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
splits over
E
, and so
\tilde{K}\leq E
. Choose
g\in\mathbf{G}(E)
such that
\mathbf{T}={{}^{g}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Let
w
denote the image of
g^{-1}\sigma(g)
in
\tilde{W}
. If
E=k_{\dtame}^{\sigma^{j}}
, then
\left|\Gal(\tilde{K}/K)\right|
divides
j
and
w^{j}
is the image of

(g^{-1}\sigma(g))^{j}=(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma^{2}(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\cdots\sigma^{(j-1)}(g^{-1}\sigma(g))=g^{-1}\sigma^{j}(g)=1

in
\tilde{W}
. Thus, the order of
w\bar{\sigma}\in\tilde{W}\times\tilde{\Gamma}
divides
j
, hence
w
is tame. Let
c
denote the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
w
. Note that
c\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}
and
\varphi_{\sigma}(c)=\mathcal{O}
.
∎

Definition 3.4.2
.

We will say that
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-tame
provided that
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
.

Corollary 3.4.3
.

Recall that we are assuming that
\tilde{K}
is a tame extension of
K
.
We have

\mathbf{G}
is
K
-tame if and only if
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}=\tilde{W}
. Moreover, if
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-tame, then the map
\varphi_{\sigma}\colon\tilde{W}_{\sim_{\sigma}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is bijective.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.4.1
it is enough to prove that
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-tame if and only if
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}=\tilde{W}
.

If
K
is quasi-finite, then this is
[
13
, Lemma 4.2.1]
. Thus, for the remainder of the proof we may and do assume that
{\mathfrak{f}}
is finite.

We first show that we may assume that
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split. Indeed,
\mathbf{G}
is an inner form of a
k
-quasi-split group
\mathbf{G}^{*}
. This means there is a
\bar{k}
-isomorphism
\varphi\colon\mathbf{G}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}
such that for all
\gamma\in\Gal(\bar{k}/k)
there exists
g_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{G}^{*}(\bar{k})
such that
c_{\gamma}=\varphi^{-1}\circ\gamma\circ\varphi=\Ad(g_{\gamma})\in\mathbf{G}^{*}_{\ad}(\bar{k})
. Since
K
has cohomological dimension
\leq 1
, we may assume that
c_{\gamma}=1\in G^{*}_{\ad}
for all
\gamma\in I=\Gal(\bar{k}/K)
. Thus
\gamma(\varphi)=\varphi
for all
\gamma\in I
and we conclude that
\varphi\colon\mathbf{G}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}
is a
K
-isomorphism that identifies
G^{*}
with
G
. Thus, without loss of generality, we may replace
\mathbf{G}
with
\mathbf{G}^{*}
and assume
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split.

Suppose
\tilde{W}=\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
. Suppose
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{C}
. The action of
\Gal(\bar{k}/k)
on
\mathbf{T}
factors through
\tilde{W}\rtimes\Aut(\tilde{\Pi})\cong N_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\rtimes\Aut(\tilde{\Pi})
. Since
\tilde{W}=\tilde{W}^{\dtame}
, we conclude that
p
does not divide the order of
\tilde{W}\rtimes\Aut(\tilde{\Pi})
, hence
\Gal(\bar{k}/k_{\dtame})
acts trivially on
\mathbf{T}
. That is,
\mathbf{T}
splits over a tame extension of
K
and
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{\dtame}
. It will be enough to show that if
p\mid\left|\tilde{W}^{\dtame}\right|
, then there is a
K
-torus in

\mathbf{G}
that doesn’t split over
k_{\dtame}
. Since we are assuming that
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split, this follows from Part 1. of
[
16
, Corollary 2.6]
.
∎

Remark 3.4.4
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{f}}
is a finite field. In
[
16
, Theorem 2.4]
Fintzen explores the extent to which the condition
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}=\tilde{W}
can be relaxed when we are interested in maximal
k
-tori rather than maximal
K
-tori. One can think of this as asking: when does the
G
-orbit of a maximal
K
-torus of
\mathbf{G}
that splits over a tame extension of
K
contain a maximal
k
-torus of
\mathbf{G}
? This question is addressed in Sections
4
and
5
.

Remark 3.4.5
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{f}}
is a finite field.
In the notation of the proof of Corollary
3.4.3
we have for all
\gamma\in\Gal(\bar{k}/K)
that

\Ad(g_{{\Fro}})=c_{{\Fro}}=\varphi^{-1}\circ{\Fro}\circ(\varphi\circ\Ad({g}_{{\Fro}^{-1}\gamma{\Fro}}))=c_{\gamma{\Fro}}=c_{\gamma}\gamma(c_{{\Fro}})=\Ad({g}_{\gamma})\circ\gamma(\Ad({g}_{\Fro}))=\gamma(\Ad({g}_{{\Fro}})).

Here we are using that
{\Fro}
normalizes
\Gal(\bar{k}/K)
and
\Ad(g_{\gamma})=1\in G^{*}_{\ad}
for all
\gamma\in\Gal(\bar{k}/K)
. Thus we have
\Ad({g}_{{\Fro}})\in G^{*}_{\ad}
. Moreover, as groups with
\Gal(K/k)
-action we can identify
G
with
G^{*}
where
{\Fro}
acts on
G^{*}
by
\Ad(g_{\Fro})\circ{\Fro}
. Since
{\mathfrak{f}}
is finite, there is an alcove
D
in
{\mathcal{B}}(G^{*})={\mathcal{B}}(G)
that is
\Ad(g_{\Fro})\circ{\Fro}
-stable. Choose
h\in G^{*}
such that
hC=D
. Since
c_{\Fro}\in\cohom^{1}({\Fro},G^{*}_{\ad})
is cohomologous to
\Ad(h^{-1})c_{\Fro}\Ad({\Fro}(h))
, we may replace
\Ad(g_{\Fro})
with
\Ad(h^{-1})\Ad(g_{\Fro})\Ad({\Fro}(h))
and assume
\Ad(g_{\Fro})
stabilizes
C
. That is, at the level of
K
-points all inner forms of
\mathbf{G}
can be thought of as
G^{*}
with
{\Fro}
acting by
\Ad(g)\circ{\Fro}
for some
\Ad(g)\in\Stab_{G^{*}_{\ad}}(C)
. Moreover, one can show that for every
\Ad(g)\in\Stab_{G^{*}_{\ad}}(C)
, the group
G^{*}
with
{\Fro}
acting by
\Ad(g)\circ{\Fro}
defines an inner form of
\mathbf{G}
. Since, by Lang-Steinberg, we have
(G_{\ad})_{C,0}=(1-\Ad(g){\Fro})(G_{\ad})_{C,0}
, it follows that every element of the coset
\Ad(g)(G_{\ad})_{C,0}
is cohomologous to
\Ad(g)
. So, for example,
\Ad(g)
is cohomologous to an element of
N_{G_{\ad}}(\mathbf{A})
: since
C\subset\mathcal{A}(\Ad(g)\cdot A)
, there exists
\Ad(k)\in(G_{\ad})_{C,0}
such that
(\Ad(g)\Ad(k))\cdot\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{A}
.

3.5.
Kac coordinates

As discussed in Remark
3.2.7
the
element
\lambda_{n}\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
encodes
Kac coordinates
of the image of
n\sigma
in
\Aut({\mathfrak{g}})
. We illustrate how this works for an
n
that is
\sigma
-elliptic.

The point
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}=\lambda_{n}(\pi)\cdot x_{0}
is an element of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(A)
and so there exists
d\in N_{G}(\mathbf{A})
such that
y_{n}:=d\cdot x_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
. Note that
\psi(x_{n})=\psi(x_{0})+\langle\dot{\psi},\lambda_{n}\rangle/\ell_{n}\in\mathbb{Q}
for all
\psi\in\Psi
. Thus, it makes sense to define
j_{n}
to be the least natural number for which
\psi(x_{n})\in\frac{1}{j_{n}}\mathbb{Z}
for all
\psi\in\Psi
. The
\left|\Delta\right|
-tuple
(j_{n}\cdot\psi(y_{n})\,|\,\psi\in\Delta)
defines
Kac coordinates
of
n
. If the point
x_{n}
belongs to the closure of
C^{\prime}
, then the coordinates are called
normalized Kac coordinates
. Tables of some Kac coordinates (or, equivalently, Kac diagrams) and values of
j_{n}
may be found in
[
1
]
,
[
9
]
,
[
29
]
,
[
38
]
,
[
40
]
, and
[
41
]
. An exhaustive list of Kac diagrams for
\sigma
-elliptic Weyl group elements may be produced by combining the results of
[
1
, §9]
and
[
15
, §4]
.

Thanks to Lemmas
2.2.2
and
3.3.6
, since
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic we have that
x_{n}
is uniquely determined, up to the action of
N_{G}(\mathbf{A})
, by
j_{n}
and the Kac coordinates of
n
. In fact, we can do better as the following lemma shows (see also
[
1
, Lemma 6.4]
).

Lemma 3.5.1
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame and
\sigma
-elliptic. We can choose
\lambda_{n}\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\cap\mathbf{A})
such that
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
. In fact, this uniquely identifies
\lambda_{n}
.

Proof.

Choose
d\in N_{G}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
y_{n}=d\cdot x_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
.
Write
d=am
where
m\in N_{G_{x_{0},0}}(\mathbf{A})
and
a\in{A}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
.
The element
a
acts on
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(A)
by translation, say
\mu\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{A})
. Then
y_{n}=am\cdot x_{n}=x_{0}+\mu+m\cdot\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}
. After replacing the
h
and
\lambda_{n}
that are used to define
g_{n}
at the start of Section
3.3
by
hm^{-1}
and
{m}\cdot\lambda_{n}
, we may assume that
y_{n}=x_{0}+(\ell_{n}\mu+\lambda_{n})/\ell_{n}
. Since
(\ell_{n}\mu+\lambda_{n})(\xi)=\lambda_{n}(\xi)
, we may also replace
\lambda_{n}
with
\ell_{n}\mu+\lambda_{n}
. Thus,
y_{n}=x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
.

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is in
\tilde{G}_{x_{0},0}\cap\tilde{G}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{G}^{\prime}
, we can carry out all of the proofs involving
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
,
\lambda_{n}
, etc. inside of
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
. In particular, we can assume
\lambda_{n}\in\X_{*}(\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\cap\mathbf{A})
. The statement about uniqueness follows immediately from the fact that since
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, we have that the
G
-orbit of
x_{n}
intersects
\bar{C}^{\prime}
exactly once.
∎

When
\lambda_{n}
is chosen such that
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
, it is
often, but not always, true that we can improve on Lemma
3.3.12
by identifying
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
with
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}^{\lambda_{n}}
. An example where we can’t identify them is
discussed in Example
3.6.3
: for the group
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
and the element
-1\in A_{1}\times\tilde{A}_{1}
in the Weyl group we have
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathrm{SO}}_{4}
while
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}^{\lambda_{n}}
is isomorphic to
\GL_{2}
. In Lemma
3.5.3
we discuss conditions under which we can identify
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
with
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}^{\lambda_{n}}
.

Definition 3.5.2
.

For a facet
F
in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(A)
, let
\mathbf{M}(F)
denote
the Levi
K
-subgroup generated by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
and the root groups
\mathbf{U}_{\alpha}
where
\alpha
runs over those roots in
\Phi(\mathbf{G},{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
for which there exists
\psi\in\Psi
such that
\dot{\psi}=\res_{\mathbf{A}}\alpha
and
\psi
is constant on
F
.

Lemma 3.5.3
.

Suppose
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
is tame and
\sigma
-elliptic. Choose
\lambda_{n}
such that
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}\in\bar{C}^{\prime}
.
Let
F_{n}
denote the facet in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(A)
to which
x_{n}
belongs. If
F_{n}
contains
x_{0}
in its closure,
then we can identify
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
with
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}^{\lambda_{n}}
via the equality
G_{x_{0},0}^{\lambda_{n}}=M(F_{n})_{x_{n},0}
.

Proof.

If
x_{0}=\bar{F}_{n}
, then since
x_{0}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}
, we conclude that
\lambda_{n}
is central. The result follows.

Suppose
x_{0}\neq\bar{F}_{n}
. Since
F_{n}
contains
x_{n}=x_{0}+\lambda_{n}/\ell_{n}
we conclude that the intersection of the ray
\vec{r}=\{x_{0}+r\lambda_{n}\,|\,r\geq 0\}
with
F_{n}
contains an open segment of
\vec{r}
.
Thus
M(F_{n})=G^{\lambda_{n}}
and so
M(F_{n})_{x_{n},0}=G_{x_{0},0}^{\lambda_{n}}
. Since the reductive quotient of
M(F_{n})_{x_{n},0}
is
\mathsf{G}_{x_{n}}
, the result follows.
∎

3.6.
Examples

We illustrate the results of this section by looking at a number of examples. Since the tori of interest depend on the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of the image of
n
in
\tilde{W}
(rather than
n
), below we label the points and tori that occur by their corresponding
\sigma
-conjugacy class in
\tilde{W}
. Also, recall that when illustrating the various
x_{T}
that arise, the isogeny class of
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
doesn’t matter (see Remark
2.2.1
).

Example 3.6.1
.

Suppose
\mathbf{G}
has
\Phi\cong\tilde{\Phi}\cong A_{n-1}
and either
{\mathfrak{f}}
has characteristic zero or
p
does not divide
n
. Fix a basis
\Pi=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{n-1}\}
for the root system of
\mathbf{G}
with respect to the torus
\mathbf{A}
. Let
w=\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}w_{i}
denote a Coxeter element where
w_{i}
is the simple reflection in
\tilde{W}
corresponding to
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi
. Let
c
denote the
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy class of
w
in
\tilde{W}
and let
\mathcal{O}_{c}
denote the corresponding
G
-conjugacy class of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
\mathbf{G}
. The barycenter of
C^{\prime}
is the unique point in the alcove
C^{\prime}
corresponding to
\mathcal{O}_{c}
.

Example 3.6.2
.

Suppose either
{\mathfrak{f}}
has characteristic zero or
p>2
and
\mathbf{G}
has type
C_{2}
. Without loss of generality we assume
\mathbf{G}
is
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
.
For each
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(K)
-conjugacy class of
K
-anisotropic maximal tori in
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(K)
we identify, in Figure
1
, the unique point in an alcove corresponding to that class.

Fix a basis
\Pi=\{\alpha,\beta\}
for the root system of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
with respect to the torus
\mathbf{A}
. Assume
\alpha
is the short root. The (absolutely) special vertex
x_{0}
of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(K)
in Figure
1
lies on hyperplanes defined by affine roots with gradients
\alpha
and
\beta
, and the pictured alcove is the unique alcove that contains
x_{0}
in its closure and on which these affine roots are positive. If
w_{\alpha}
and
w_{\beta}
denote the simple reflections corresponding to
\alpha
and
\beta
, then the two elliptic conjugacy classes in the Weyl group are
C_{2}=\{w_{\alpha}w_{\beta},w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}\}
and
-1\in A_{1}\times{A}_{1}=\{w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}\}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
denote the corresponding
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(K)
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
. Since the square of every element in
C_{2}
is
-1
, we can read off the unique points in the alcove that correspond to
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
from
[
40
, Section 8.2.2]
.
For
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
the point is
x_{C_{2}}=x_{0}+(3\check{\alpha}+4\check{\beta})/8
, and for
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
the point is
x_{-1}=x_{0}+(\check{\alpha}+2\check{\beta})/4
.

x_{0}
x_{C_{2}}
x_{-1}

K
-minisotropic maximal tori in type
C_{2}

Example 3.6.3
.

Suppose either
{\mathfrak{f}}
has characteristic zero or
p>3
. For each
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(K)
-conjugacy class of

-anisotropic maximal tori in
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
we identify, in Figure

Fix a basis
\Pi=\{\alpha,\beta\}
for the root system of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
with respect to the torus
\mathbf{A}
. Assume
\alpha
is the short root. The special vertex
x_{0}
of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(K)
in Figure
2
lies on hyperplanes defined by affine roots with gradients
\alpha
and
\beta
, and the pictured alcove is the unique alcove that contains
x_{0}
in its closure and on which these affine roots are positive. If
w_{\alpha}
and
w_{\beta}
denote the simple reflections corresponding to
\alpha
and
\beta
, then the three elliptic conjugacy classes in the Weyl group are
G_{2}=\{w_{\alpha}w_{\beta},w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}\}
,
A_{2}=\{w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta},w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}\}
and
-1\in A_{1}\times\tilde{A}_{1}=\{w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}\}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{G_{2}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
denote the corresponding
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(K)
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
. From
[
41
, Section 5.1]
the unique points in the alcove that correspond to
\mathcal{O}_{G_{2}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
are:
x_{G_{2}}=x_{0}+(3\check{\alpha}+5\check{\beta})/6
;
x_{A_{2}}=x_{0}+(\check{\alpha}+2\check{\beta})/3
; and
x_{-1}=x_{0}+(\check{\alpha}+2\check{\beta})/2
.

x_{-1}

K
-minisotropic maximal tori in
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}

Example 3.6.4
.

Suppose
p>3
and
\mathbf{G}
has type
{{}^{2}\!}A_{2}
. Without loss of generality we assume
\mathbf{G}
is simply connected.
For each
G
-conjugacy class of
K
-anisotropic maximal tori in a ramified
\SU(3)
we identify, in Figure
3
, the unique point in an alcove corresponding to that class.
Thinking of
\SU(3,K)
as the
\sigma
-fixed points of
\SL_{3}(\tilde{K})
, the dotted equilateral triangle is a
\sigma
-stable alcove of
\SL_{3}(\tilde{K})
. If
\tilde{\Pi}=\{\alpha,\beta\}
, then
\sigma(\alpha)=\beta
.
The absolutely special vertex,
x_{0}
, of
\SU(3)
pictured in Figure
3
lies on the hyperplanes defined by affine roots with gradients
\alpha
and
\beta
while the other vertex,
z
, lies on a hyperplane defined by an affine root with gradient
\alpha+\beta
. Since
p\neq 2
, the reductive quotient at
x_{0}
is
\PGL_{2}
and at
z
it is
\SL_{2}
.

The
\sigma
-conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}
are
c_{1}=\{1,w_{\alpha}w_{\beta},w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}\}
,
c_{w_{\alpha}}=\{w_{\alpha},w_{\beta}\}
, and

Remark 3.6.5
.

In at least one respect, Example
3.6.4
is a little misleading. In general, facets do not behave well under extensions that are not unramified. For example, for ramified
\SU(4)
an alcove over
K
intersects multiple alcoves over
\tilde{K}
. It can also happen, even when
k
is residually quasi-split, that an alcove over
k
is not contained in any alcove over a totally ramified splitting extension (consider, for example,
\SL_{1}(D)
for
D
an index two division algebra over
k
).

Example 3.6.6
.

Suppose either
{\mathfrak{f}}
has characteristic zero or
p>3
. Suppose
\mathbf{G}
is a group of type

. For each

, the unique point in an alcove corresponding to that class.

.
