\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
denote the maximal torus in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
corresponding to
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. That is, the group of
\mathfrak{F}
-points of
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
coincides with the image of
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{c})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{F_{c},0}
in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
.

If
\mathsf{T}
is a maximal
\mathfrak{F}
-torus in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
then there exists
g\in\mathsf{G}_{c}
such that
\mathsf{T}={{}^{g}\!}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. If
\mathsf{T}
is
\sigma
-stable, then
g^{-1}\sigma(g)\in N_{\mathsf{G}_{c}}(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
determines a
\sigma
-conjugacy class in
\tilde{W}
. This gives a well-defined map from the set of
\sigma
-stable maximal tori in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
to
\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{c}
denote the preimage of
c
under this map, and let
\mathcal{O}^{{\Fro}}_{c}
denote the subset of
\mathcal{O}_{c}
consisting of tori that are
{\Fro}
-stable. Note that
\mathsf{G}_{x_{c}}
(
=G_{x_{c},0}/G_{x_{c},0^{+}}
) acts on
\mathcal{O}_{c}
while
\mathsf{G}_{x_{c}}^{\Fro}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{c}
and
\mathcal{O}^{{\Fro}}_{c}
.

Recall from Definition
6.3.12
that
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}
denotes the set of
{\Fro}
-stable tori in
\Ad(G_{x_{c},0})\mathbf{T}^{c}
. For
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}
let
\varphi(\mathbf{T})\in\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
be the torus whose group of
\mathfrak{F}
-points corresponds to the image of
\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0}\cap\mathbf{T}(K_{c})
in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
. In this way we define a
G_{x_{c},0}^{\Fro}
-equivariant map

\varphi\colon\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
.

Lemma 7.2.2
.

If
\mathsf{T}\in\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
and
\mathbf{T}_{1},\mathbf{T}_{2}\in\varphi^{-1}[\mathsf{T}]
, then
\mathbf{T}_{1}
and
\mathbf{T}_{2}
are
G^{{\Fro}}_{x_{c},0^{+}}
-conjugate.

With minor changes (e.g., changing
K
to
K_{c}
and
\Gamma
to
\Gal(K_{c}/k)
), this proof is the same as the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 2.2.2]
.

Proof.

From Lemma
7.2.1
there exists
g\in G(K_{c})_{x_{c},0^{+}}
such that
\mathbf{T}_{2}={{}^{g}\!}\mathbf{T}_{1}
.
For
\gamma\in\Gal(K_{c}/k)
, let
c_{g}(\gamma):=g^{-1}\gamma(g)\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{c})}(\mathbf{T}_{1})
;
c_{g}
is a one-cocycle.
Since
F_{c}
is
\Gal(K_{c}/k)
-stable and

g\in\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0^{+}}
, we have
c_{g}(\gamma)\in\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0^{+}}
.
From Lemma
7.2.1
we conclude
that
c_{g}(\gamma)\in\mathbf{T}_{1}(K_{c})_{0^{+}}
.

Since
\cohom^{1}(\Gal(K_{c}/k),\mathbf{T}_{1}(K_{c})_{0^{+}})

is trivial
[
25
, Theorem 13.8.5 (1)]
, there exists a
z\in\mathbf{T}_{1}(K_{c})_{0^{+}}

such
that
gz
is fixed by
\Gal(K_{c}/k)
. We have
{{}^{gz}\!}\mathbf{T}_{1}=\mathbf{T}_{2}
and, thanks to
[
25
, Proposition 12.9.4]
,
gz\in\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0^{+}}^{\Gal(K_{c}/k)}=G_{x_{c},0^{+}}^{{\Fro}}
.
∎

Lemma 7.2.3
.

The
G_{x_{c},0}^{\Fro}
-equivariant map

\varphi\colon\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
is surjective.

Proof.

Fix
\mathsf{T}\in\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
. Since
\mathsf{T}\in\mathcal{O}_{c}
, there exists
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0}
such that
\mathbf{T}={{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}_{c}
is a lift of
\mathsf{T}
; that is,
\mathsf{T}
corresponds to the image of
\mathbf{T}(K_{c})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{c})_{x_{c},0}
in
\mathsf{G}_{c}
.

Let
E\leq K_{c}
be a finite Galois extension of
k
such that
\mathbf{T}
is
E
-split. It will be enough to show that there exists
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
such that
{{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}
is a
k
-torus.

Suppose that for all
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
we have that
{{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}
is not a
k
-torus.
Then, thanks to Lemma
7.2.1
, for all
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
there exists
s_{\ell}\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that

•

\gamma({{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T})
is contained in the
{\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{\ell}}}
-orbit of
{{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}
for all
\gamma\in\Gal(E/k)
and

•

there exists
\gamma\in\Gal(E/k)
such that
\gamma({{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T})
is not contained in the
{\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{\ell}^{+}}}
-orbit of
{{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}

Since
\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
is compact and the assignment
\ell\mapsto s_{\ell}
is locally constant, there exists
h\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
such that
s_{h}\geq s_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
.
Without loss of generality, we set
\mathbf{T}={{}^{h}\!}\mathbf{T}
. We then have
s_{1}\geq s_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
.
We will produce
j\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},0^{+}}
such that
s_{j}>s_{1}
, a contradiction.

If
\gamma,\rho\in\Gal(E/k)
, then
\gamma\rho(\mathbf{T})={{}^{\ell_{\gamma\rho}}\!}\mathbf{T}=\gamma({{}^{\ell_{\rho}}\!}\mathbf{T})={{}^{\gamma(\ell_{\rho})\ell_{\gamma}}\!}\mathbf{T}
.
Hence
f_{\mathbf{T}}(\gamma,\rho):=\ell_{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot\gamma(\ell_{\rho})^{-1}\cdot\ell_{\gamma\rho}
is an element of
\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}}\cap N_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbf{T})
. From Lemma
7.2.1
we conclude that
f_{\mathbf{T}}(\gamma,\rho)\in\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}}
. Since
\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
is abelian, one checks that the induced function
\bar{f}\colon\Gal(E/k)\times\Gal(E/k)\rightarrow\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
is a
2
-cocycle. Since
\cohom^{2}(\Gal(E/k),\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}})
is trivial
[
25
, Theorem 13.8.5 (2)]
, there exists
\bar{\alpha}\colon\Gal(E/k)\rightarrow\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
, written
\gamma\mapsto\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
, such that
\bar{f}(\gamma,\sigma)=\gamma\bar{\alpha}_{\sigma}\cdot\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma\sigma}^{-1}\cdot\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
for all
\gamma,\sigma\in\Gal(E/k)
. If
\bar{\ell}_{\gamma}
denotes the image of
\ell_{\gamma}
in
\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
, then one checks that
\gamma\mapsto\bar{\ell}_{\gamma}\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
defines a
1
-cocycle with values in
\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
. Since
\cohom^{1}(\Gal(E/k),\mathbf{G}(E)_{s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}})
is trivial
[
25
, Theorem 13.8.5 (2)]
, there exists
j\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}}
such that
the image of
\gamma(j)^{-1}j
in
\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}:s_{1}^{+}}
is
\bar{\ell}_{\gamma}\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
for all
\gamma\in\Gal(E/k)
. If
\alpha_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{T}(E)_{s_{1}}
is any lift of
\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
then there exists
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}^{+}}
such that
\ell_{\gamma}\alpha_{\gamma}=\gamma(j)^{-1}jx_{\gamma}
. Thus, for all
\gamma\in\Gal(E/k)
we have

\gamma({{}^{j}\!}\mathbf{T})={{}^{\gamma(j)\ell_{\gamma}}\!}\mathbf{T}={{}^{jx_{\gamma}\alpha_{\gamma}^{-1}}\!}\mathbf{T}={{}^{jx_{\gamma}j^{-1}}\!}({{}^{j}\!}\mathbf{T}).

Since
jx_{\gamma}j^{-1}\in\mathbf{G}(E)_{x_{c},s_{1}^{+}}
, we conclude that
s_{j}
is greater than
s_{1}
.
∎

Corollary 7.2.4
.

The
\mathsf{G}_{x_{c}}^{\Fro}
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
are in bijective correspondence with the
G^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c}}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
7.2.3
and Lemma
7.2.2

the
G_{x_{c},0}^{\Fro}
-equivariant map
\varphi\colon\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}

descends to a bijective map from the set of

G_{x_{c},0}^{\Fro}
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathbf{T}^{c},x_{c}}

to the set of

\mathsf{G}_{x_{c}}^{\Fro}
-conjugacy classes in

\mathcal{O}_{c}^{{\Fro}}
. The result now follows from
Corollary
6.3.13
.
∎

7.3.
A more concrete realization of
W_{T}
,
T/T_{0^{+}}
,
T_{F}/T_{0^{+}}
, and
\bar{W}_{T}
in the tame setting

Fix
c\in\tilde{W}^{\dtame}_{\sim_{\sigma}}
,
w\in c
,
n\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
with image
w
in
\tilde{W}
.
Choose
h
,
\lambda=\lambda_{n}
,
g=g_{n}\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}
as in §
3.3
and set
\mathbf{T}={{}^{g}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
. Note that the image of
n_{\sigma}:=g^{-1}\sigma(g)
in
\tilde{W}
is
w
. Let
F\subset{\mathcal{B}}(G)
be the facet corresponding to
x_{n}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\red}(G)
.

Because it will help with the bookkeeping later, we will refer to
w
as
w_{\sigma}
.

Lemma 7.3.1
.

We have

(1)

The group homomorphism that sends
m\in N_{G}(\mathbf{T})
to
g^{-1}mg\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
induces an isomorphism

\rho_{g}\colon N_{G}(\mathbf{T})/T\longrightarrow\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}.

(2)

The group homomorphism that sends
t\in T
to
g^{-1}tg\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
induces an isomorphism

\rho_{g}\colon T/{T_{0^{+}}}\longrightarrow({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}.

(3)

The group homomorphism that sends
t\in T_{F}
to
g^{-1}tg\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0}
induces an injective map

\rho_{g}\colon T_{F}/{T_{0^{+}}}\longrightarrow(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}.

(4)

If
w_{\sigma}
is
\sigma
-elliptic, then for
g
as in Lemma
3.3.9
the group homomorphism that sends
m\in N_{G_{F,0}}(T)
to
g^{-1}mg\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
induces an injective map

\rho_{g}\colon N_{G_{F,0}}(\mathbf{T})/T_{0^{+}}\longrightarrow(N_{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}}(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n\lambda(\xi)\sigma}.

Moreover, if
\mathbf{G}
is simply connected, then
T_{0}=T_{0^{+}}
,
\bar{W}_{T}=N_{G_{F,0}}(\mathbf{T})/T_{0^{+}}
, and the maps of both (
3
) and (
4
) are isomorphisms.

Remark 7.3.2
.

The maps in (
3
) and (
4
) both fail to be surjective when
\mathbf{G}=\PGL_{2}
. See also Lemma
7.4.9
.

Proof.

Recall that
{n_{\sigma}}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
has image
w_{\sigma}
in
\tilde{W}
.

We begin by showing (
1
).
Suppose
m\in N_{G}(\mathbf{T})
.
Since

{n_{\sigma}}\sigma(g^{-1}mg){n_{\sigma}}^{-1}=(g^{-1}\sigma(g))(\sigma(g^{-1})m\sigma(g))(\sigma(g^{-1})g)=g^{-1}mg,

the map sending
m\in N_{G}(\mathbf{T})
to
g^{-1}mg\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
induces a homomorphism from
N_{G}(\mathbf{T})
to
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
. Since the kernel of this homomorphism is
T
, the map descends to an injective map
\rho_{g}\colon W_{\mathbf{T}}\rightarrow\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

We now show that
\rho_{g}
is surjective. Choose
w^{\prime}\in\tilde{W}^{w\sigma}
. Fix
n^{\prime}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that the image of
n^{\prime}
in
\tilde{W}
is
w^{\prime}
. We have

\sigma({{}^{g}\!}n^{\prime})=g(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma(n^{\prime})(\sigma(g)^{-1}g)g^{-1}=g({{}^{n_{\sigma}}\!}\sigma(n^{\prime}))g^{-1}={{}^{g}\!}(n^{\prime}a)={{}^{g}\!}n^{\prime}{{}^{g}\!}a

for some
a\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
.
As
{{}^{g}\!}a\in\mathbf{T}(K_{n})
we conclude that the coset
({{}^{g}\!}n^{\prime})\mathbf{T}(K_{n})
is
\sigma
-invariant. Since
\coho^{1}(\sigma,\mathbf{T}(K_{n}))
is trivial, we conclude that every
\sigma
-invariant coset in
N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}(K_{n})
has a representative in
N_{G}(\mathbf{T})/T
. Hence,
\rho_{g}
is surjective.

We now show that (
2
) holds. Note that

\begin{split}T_{0^{+}}&=T\cap G_{F,0^{+}}=\mathbf{T}(K_{n})^{\sigma}\cap(\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}})^{\sigma}=(\mathbf{T}(K_{n})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}})^{\sigma}\\
&=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n}))\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}})^{\sigma}=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}}))^{\sigma}=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{\sigma}.\end{split}

Since
\coho^{1}(\sigma,\mathbf{T}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})
is trivial, we conclude that
T/T_{0^{+}}
is isomorphic to

({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n}))/{{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{\sigma}=({{}^{{g}}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})))^{\sigma}.

Since
w_{\sigma}
is the image of
g^{-1}\sigma(g)
in
\tilde{W}
, we conclude that
({{}^{{g}}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})))^{\sigma}
is isomorphic, via their identification under the map
\Ad(g)
, to
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

For (
3
), recall that
T_{F}=T\cap G_{F,0}
and
g\in G_{F,0}
, so
g^{-1}T_{F}g\leq{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0}
. Thus, from (
2
) we have an injective map from
T_{F}/{T_{0^{+}}}
to
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0}/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
. Since
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0}/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})
is isomorphic to
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
, the result follows.

We now show that (
4
) is valid. From Lemma
3.3.9
we may assume that
g^{-1}\sigma(g)=z\cdot{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}n
for some
z\in\tilde{Z}_{0}
.
Suppose
m\in N_{G_{F,0}}(\mathbf{T})
. Since
g\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}
we have
g^{-1}mg\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
. Since conjugation by
g
carries topologically unipotent elements in
G_{F,0}
to topologically unipotent elements in
\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}
, we have a well-defined injective group homomorphism from
\bar{W}_{T}
to
\Stab_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0^{+}}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
with image in
(\Stab_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0^{+}}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{{n_{\sigma}}\sigma}
. Here the stabilizer in the quotient is to be interpreted as in Remark
6.6.4
.
Since
x_{n}=\lambda(\pi)\cdot x_{0}
,
{n_{\sigma}}=z\cdot{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}n
, and
\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0^{+}}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}
the result follows.

Finally, if
\mathbf{G}
is simply connected and
x\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}
has image in
(\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0^{+}})^{n\lambda(\xi)\sigma}
, then
g\lambda(\pi)x\lambda^{-1}(\pi)g^{-1}
belongs to
\Stab_{\mathbf{G}(K)}(F)\cdot\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}}=\mathbf{G}(K)_{F,0}\cdot\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}}
. It follows that the maps of both (
3
) and (
4
) are surjective, hence isomorphisms.
∎

Remark 7.3.3
.

If
y\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})
also has the property that
{{}^{y}\!}{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}=\mathbf{T}
, then there exists a
\tilde{n}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
g=y\tilde{n}
. Thus,
\rho_{y}(N_{G}(\mathbf{T})/T)=\bar{w}\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}\bar{w}^{-1}=\tilde{W}^{(\bar{w}w_{\sigma}\sigma(\bar{w})^{-1})\sigma}
where
\bar{w}
denotes the image of
\tilde{n}
in
\tilde{W}
. Similarly,

\rho_{y}(T/T_{0^{+}})=({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{(\bar{w}w_{\sigma}\sigma(\bar{w})^{-1})\sigma}
.

7.4.
The action of
{\Fro}

Suppose
{{}^{G}\!}\mathbf{T}
contains a
k
-torus. It may happen that
\mathbf{T}={{}^{g}\!}\mathbf{A}
is not defined over
k
. However, if
{{}^{G}\!}\mathbf{T}
contains a
{\Fro}
-stable element, then there exists
\ell\in G
such that
{{}^{\ell g}\!}\mathbf{A}={{}^{\ell}\!}\mathbf{T}
is defined over
k
. In fact, if
\mathbf{T}
is
K
-minisotropic, then from Lemma
2.3.1
and Lemma
6.1.1
we can also assume
\ell\in G_{x_{n},0}
. Note that
(\ell g)^{-1}\sigma(\ell g)=g^{-1}\sigma(g)=n_{\sigma}
.

Definition 7.4.1
.

Suppose
\mathbf{T}={{}^{g}\!}\mathbf{A}
is defined over
k
and
g\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}
.
Define
n_{{\Fro}}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
by
n_{{\Fro}}:=g^{-1}{\Fro}(g)
.
Let
w_{{\Fro}}
denote the image of
n_{{\Fro}}
in
\tilde{W}
.

Lemma 7.4.2
.

We have that
n_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}
stabilizes
N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})^{n_{\sigma}\sigma}
while

w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}
stabilizes
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

Proof.

It will be enough to prove the first statement. Suppose
m\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
and
\Ad({n_{\sigma}})\sigma(m)=m
. We have

\begin{split}\Ad({n_{\sigma}})\sigma[\Ad({n_{\Fro}}){\Fro}(m)]&=\Ad[(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma(g^{-1}{\Fro}(g))]\sigma({\Fro}(m))\\
&=\Ad[(g^{-1}\sigma({\Fro}(g))]\sigma({\Fro}(m))\\
&=\Ad(n_{\Fro}){\Fro}[\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma^{q}(g))\sigma^{q}(m)]\\
\end{split}

Since

\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma^{q}(g))\sigma^{q}(m)=\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma^{q-1}(g))\sigma^{q-1}[\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma(m)]=\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma^{q-1}(g))\sigma^{q-1}[m],

after
q-1
iterations we conclude that
\Ad(g^{-1}\sigma^{q}(g))\sigma^{q}(m)=m
. Hence, we have shown

\Ad({n_{\sigma}})\sigma[\Ad({n_{\Fro}}){\Fro}(m)]=\Ad(n_{\Fro}){\Fro}(m),

as required.
∎

Lemma 7.4.3
.

Via
\Ad(g^{-1})
(that is,
\rho_{g}
), the action of
{\Fro}
on
W_{T}
and
T/T_{0^{+}}
corresponds to the action of
w_{{\Fro}}\circ{\Fro}
on
\tilde{W}^{w\sigma}
and
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})^{w\sigma}
.

Proof.

We will verify this for
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
; the proof for
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
is similar. The map from
N_{G}(T)
to
N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
is given by
m\mapsto g^{-1}mg
. Thus
{\Fro}(m)
maps to

g^{-1}{\Fro}(m)g=g^{-1}{\Fro}(gg^{-1}){\Fro}(m){\Fro}(gg^{-1})g=(g^{-1}{\Fro}(g)){\Fro}(g^{-1}mg)({\Fro}(g)^{-1}g).

The result follows from looking at the images of these elements in
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.
∎

Lemma 7.4.4
.

We have

{\Fro}(N_{q}(n_{\sigma}))=n^{-1}_{\Fro}n_{\sigma}\sigma(n_{\Fro})
and
{\Fro}(N_{q}(w_{\sigma}))=w^{-1}_{\Fro}w_{\sigma}\sigma(w_{\Fro})
.

Proof.

For the first equality we have:

\begin{split}{\Fro}(N_{q}(n_{\sigma}))&={\Fro}(n_{\sigma}\cdot\sigma(n_{\sigma})\cdots\sigma^{q-1}(n_{\sigma}))={\Fro}(g^{-1}\sigma(g)\cdot\sigma(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\cdots\sigma^{q-1}(g^{-1}\sigma(g)))\\
&={\Fro}(g^{-1}\sigma^{q}(g))={\Fro}(g^{-1}({\Fro}^{-1}\circ\sigma\circ{\Fro})(g))={\Fro}(g^{-1})\sigma({\Fro}(g))\\
&={\Fro}(g^{-1})(gg^{-1})\sigma((gg^{-1}){\Fro}(g))=({\Fro}(g^{-1})g)(g^{-1}\sigma(g))\sigma(g^{-1}{\Fro}(g))\\
&=n^{-1}_{\Fro}n_{\sigma}\sigma(n_{\Fro}).\end{split}

The second equality follows from the first.
∎

Corollary 7.4.5
.

Recall that
n_{\Fro}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
.
The equality

{\Fro}(N_{q}(n_{\sigma}))=n^{-1}_{\Fro}n_{\sigma}\sigma(n_{\Fro})

of Lemma
7.4.4

uniquely determines the element
n_{\Fro}
up to left multiplication by an element of
(N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n_{\sigma}\circ\sigma}
. Similarly, the equality

{\Fro}(N_{q}(w_{\sigma}))=w^{-1}_{\Fro}w_{\sigma}\sigma(w_{\Fro})

uniquely determines the element
w_{\Fro}
up to left multiplication by an element of
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

Proof.

It will be enough to establish the first statement. Suppose
y\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
satisfies

{\Fro}(N_{q}(n_{\sigma}))=y^{-1}n_{\sigma}\sigma(y).

We then have

n_{\sigma}\sigma(n_{{\Fro}}y^{-1})n_{\sigma}^{-1}=n_{\Fro}y^{-1}

and the result follows.
∎

Lemma 7.4.6
.

Suppose
c
is
\sigma
-elliptic. Suppose
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}={{}^{h}\!}\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
with
h\in G
. Set
m_{h}=h^{-1}{\Fro}(h)\in N_{G}(\mathbf{T})
and let
w_{h}
denote the image of
g^{-1}{m}_{h}g
in
\tilde{W}
. Via the map
\Ad(g^{-1})\circ\Ad(h^{-1})
( i.e., the composition
\rho_{g}\circ\varphi_{h}
where
\varphi_{h}
is defined in Lemma
6.4.12
) the action of
{\Fro}
on
W_{T}
and
T/T_{0^{+}}
corresponds to the action of
(w_{h}w_{{\Fro}})\circ{\Fro}
on
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
and
({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
6.4.12
and
7.4.3
.
∎

Remark 7.4.7
.

If
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-split, then from Lemmas
7.4.3
and
7.4.4
we have
{\Fro}(w_{\sigma}^{q})=w^{-1}_{\Fro}w_{\sigma}w_{\Fro}
and
(W_{T})_{\sim_{{\Fro}}}
is in bijection with the set of
w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}
-conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}
.

Remark 7.4.8
.

Note that from Corollary
7.4.5
the element
w_{\Fro}
will only be determined (in
\tilde{W}
) up to left multiplication by an element of
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}
, but from Lemma
7.4.6
this is exactly what must happen as different choices of base point for
{{}^{G}\!}\mathbf{T}
will result in different realizations of the various actions.

Lemma 7.4.9
.

Suppose
\mathbf{G}
is semisimple and
c
is
\sigma
-elliptic.
The group homomorphism that sends
t\in T
to
g^{-1}tg\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})
induces an isomorphism
\rho_{g}\colon T/{T_{0^{+}}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
.

Remark 7.4.10
.

If
\mathbf{G}
is simply connected, then since
K_{n}/K
is tame we have
\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}^{\sigma}=G_{F,0}
. Thus, in this case,

\mathsf{T}_{F}=T/{T_{0^{+}}}
and so
\mathsf{T}_{F}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
in agreement with Lemma
7.3.1
.

Proof.

Since
c
is
\sigma
-elliptic, we have that
T
is a bounded subgroup of
G
. It follows that
T
is bounded in
\mathbf{T}(K_{n})
, and so
T\subset\mathbf{T}(R_{K_{n}})
where
R_{K_{n}}
denotes the ring of integers of
K_{n}
. Since
g\in\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}
, we have

T=\mathbf{T}(R_{K_{n}})^{\sigma}=(\mathbf{T}(K_{n})\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0})^{\sigma}=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n}))\cap\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0})^{\sigma}=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(R_{K_{n}})))^{\sigma}.

From the proof of Lemma
7.3.1
we have

T_{0^{+}}=({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{\sigma}
.
Identify
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}
with the image of
{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(R_{K_{n}})/{\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}
in
\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}}
. Since
\coho^{1}(\sigma,T_{0^{+}})
is trivial, we conclude that
T/T_{0^{+}}
is isomorphic to

({{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(R_{K_{n}})/{{}^{g}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}}))^{\sigma}=({{}^{\bar{g}}\!}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(R_{K_{n}})/({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}(K_{n})_{0^{+}})))^{\sigma}=({{}^{\bar{g}}\!}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})^{\sigma}

where
\bar{g}
denotes the image of
g
in
\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0}/\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{F,0^{+}}
.

Since
w_{\sigma}
denotes the image of
g^{-1}\sigma(g)\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
in
\tilde{W}
, we have that
({{}^{\bar{g}}\!}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})^{\sigma}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
via their identification under the map
\Ad(\bar{g})
.
∎

Corollary 7.4.11
.

Suppose
\mathbf{G}
is simply connected and
w_{\sigma}
is
\sigma
-elliptic. The set of
k
-stable classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
is indexed by
(\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma})_{\sim_{w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}}}
. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
is a
k
-torus in the
w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}
-class corresponding to
w^{\prime}\in\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
, then, up to
G^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy, the set of
k
-embeddings of
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
into
\mathbf{G}
is indexed by
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}_{w^{\prime}w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
6.2.7
,
6.4.10
,
7.3.1
, and
7.4.3
.
∎

7.5.
The action of
{\Fro}
when
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic

In this subsection we refine the results of subsection
7.4
under the additional assumptions that
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic.

Since
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split, we may assume that
x_{0}
is Frobenius fixed, and so, since
x_{n}
is
{\Fro}
-invariant, we have that
{\Fro}(\lambda)=\lambda
. In this section we also assume that
{\Fro}
is chosen such that
{\Fro}(\pi)=\pi
.

Since
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, from Lemma
3.3.9
we may assume that
n_{\sigma}=z\cdot{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}n
.
for some
z\in\tilde{Z}_{0}
.

Definition 7.5.1
.

Define
n_{F}\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
by
n_{F}={{}^{\lambda^{-1}(\pi)}\!}n_{{\Fro}}
.

Corollary 7.5.2
.

If
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, then
we have that
n_{F}\circ{\Fro}
stabilizes

N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})^{n\lambda(\xi)\sigma}
.

Proof.

Suppose
x\in N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}})
such that
{{}^{n\lambda(\xi)}\!}\sigma(x)=x
. Then

{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}x={{}^{\lambda(\pi)n\lambda(\xi)}\!}\sigma(x)={{}^{n_{\sigma}}\!}\sigma({{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}x).

Thus,
{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}x\in(N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{n},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n_{\sigma}\sigma}
.
Since
{{}^{\lambda(\pi)}\!}n_{F}\circ{\Fro}=n_{{\Fro}}\circ{\Fro}
, the result follows by unwinding definitions and using Lemma
7.4.2
.
∎

Corollary 7.5.3
.

If
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, then we have

{\Fro}[N_{q}(n\lambda(\xi)z)]=n_{F}^{-1}(n\lambda(\xi)z)\sigma(n_{F})
.

Proof.

We have

\begin{split}{\Fro}[N_{q}(n&\lambda(\xi)z)]={\Fro}[N_{q}({{}^{\lambda^{-1}(\pi)}\!}n_{\sigma}\lambda(\xi))]={\Fro}[N_{q}(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{\sigma}\sigma(\lambda(\pi)))]\\
&={\Fro}[(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{\sigma}\sigma(\lambda(\pi)))\cdot\sigma(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{\sigma}\sigma(\lambda(\pi)))\cdot\sigma^{2}(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{\sigma}\sigma(\lambda(\pi)))\cdot\cdots\cdot\sigma^{q-1}(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{\sigma}\sigma(\lambda(\pi))]\\
&=\lambda^{-1}(\pi){\Fro}[N_{q}(n_{\sigma})\sigma^{q}(\lambda(\pi))]=\lambda^{-1}(\pi){\Fro}[N_{q}(n_{\sigma})]\lambda(\pi)\cdot{\Fro}[\lambda^{-1}(\pi)\sigma^{q}(\lambda(\pi))]\\
&=\lambda^{-1}(\pi){\Fro}[N_{q}(n_{\sigma})]\lambda(\pi)\cdot\lambda({\Fro}(\xi^{q}))\end{split}

From Lemma
7.4.4
, this becomes

\begin{split}\lambda^{-1}(\pi)&n_{{\Fro}}^{-1}n_{\sigma}\sigma(n_{{\Fro}})\lambda(\pi)\cdot\lambda({\Fro}({\Fro}^{-1}(\xi)))=(n_{F}^{-1}nz)\cdot\lambda^{-1}(\pi)\sigma(n_{{\Fro}})\lambda(\pi)\cdot\lambda(\xi)\\
&=(n_{F}^{-1}nz)\cdot\lambda^{-1}(\pi)\sigma(\lambda(\pi))\cdot\sigma(\lambda^{-1}(\pi)n_{{\Fro}}\lambda(\pi))\cdot\sigma(\lambda^{-1}(\pi))\lambda(\pi)\cdot\lambda(\xi)\\
&=(n_{F}^{-1}nz\lambda(\xi))\sigma(n_{F})\cdot\lambda^{-1}(\xi)\cdot\lambda(\xi)\\
&=n_{F}^{-1}(n\lambda(\xi)z)\sigma(n_{F}).\end{split}

∎

Corollary 7.5.4
.

If
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic, then the equality

{\Fro}(N_{q}(n\lambda(\xi)z))=n^{-1}_{F}n\lambda(\xi)z\sigma(n_{F})

uniquely determines the element
n_{F}
up to left multiplication by an element of
(N_{\mathbf{G}(K_{n})_{x_{0},0}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n\lambda(\xi)\circ\sigma}
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to that of Corollary
7.4.5
.
∎

Corollary 7.5.5
.

Suppose
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-quasi-split and simply connected. Suppose also that
n
is
\sigma
-elliptic. The set of
\mathbf{G}(k)
-classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
is indexed by
((N_{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}}(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n\lambda(\xi)\sigma})_{\sim_{n_{F}\circ{\Fro}}}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.4.7
, Lemma
7.3.1
, and tracing through how
{\Fro}
acts on
(N_{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{x_{0}}}(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}))^{n\lambda(\xi)\sigma}
.
∎

7.6.
Example: the Coxeter conjugacy class for
K
-split groups

Suppose
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-split. Since
\mathbf{G}
is
K
-split, we have that
\sigma
acts trivially on
\tilde{W}
and
\Pi=\tilde{\Pi}
. Suppose
w_{\sigma}
is a Coxeter element
\prod_{\alpha\in\tilde{\Pi}}w_{\alpha}
in
\tilde{W}
; here
w_{\alpha}
is the simple reflection in
\tilde{W}
corresponding to
\alpha
.
We have that
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}=\langle w_{\sigma}\rangle
. Let
\ell
denote the order of
w_{\sigma}
and suppose throughout this section that
(p,\ell)=1
.

Since
(p,\ell)=1
, we know that
w_{\sigma}^{q}
is
\tilde{W}
-conjugate to
w_{\sigma}
. Since
{\Fro}
preserves
\tilde{\Phi}
, it must carry
\tilde{\Pi}
to another basis for
\tilde{\Phi}
. Thus
{\Fro}(w_{\sigma})
is again a Coxeter element of
\tilde{W}
.
Since the set of Coxeter elements form a single
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy class, we conclude that the Coxeter conjugacy class in
\tilde{W}
is stable under
{\Fro}\circ N_{q}
. Thus, from Corollary
5.1.3
we may choose a
{\Fro}
-stable maximal torus
\mathbf{T}
of
\mathbf{G}
in
\varphi_{\sigma}({{}^{\tilde{W}}\!}w_{\sigma})
.

From Lemma
6.4.10
we have that the elements of the set of
k
-stable conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
are indexed by
(W_{T})_{\sim_{{\Fro}}}
. Since
y=w_{\sigma}^{q}
also generates
\langle w_{\sigma}\rangle=\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}
, from Lemma
7.4.3
and the fact that
W_{T}
is abelian we have that
(W_{T})_{\sim_{{\Fro}}}
is isomorphic to

\langle w_{\sigma}\rangle/\langle y^{-1}(w_{\Fro}{\Fro}(y)w_{{\Fro}}^{-1})\rangle
. From Lemma
7.4.4
we have that

y^{-1}(w_{\Fro}{\Fro}(y)w_{{\Fro}}^{-1})=(w_{\sigma}^{-1})^{q-1}
. Thus, the elements of the set of
k
-stable conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
are indexed by
\langle w_{\sigma}\rangle/\langle w_{\sigma}^{q-1}\rangle\cong\mathbb{Z}/(\ell,q-1)
.

The group
T_{F}/T_{0}
may be identified with
\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}}
, the center of
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}
. Thus, from Lemma
6.2.7
, we conclude that, up to
G^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy, the group
(\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}})_{{\Fro}}
parameterizes the
k
-embeddings of
\mathbf{T}
into
\mathbf{G}
.

Since
\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}}
is central in the reductive quotient
\mathsf{G}_{x_{0}}
, we conclude that
w_{\sigma}
acts trivially on
\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}}
. Thus, if
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
is a
k
-torus in one of the stable classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
, then, up to
G^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy, the set of
k
-embeddings of
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
into
\mathbf{G}
is indexed by
(\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}})_{w_{h}w_{\sigma}\circ{\Fro}}\cong(\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}})_{{\Fro}}
.
If we also assume that
\mathbf{G}
is
k
-split, then
(\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}})_{{\Fro}}=\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}}/\{x^{q-1}\,|\,x\in\mathsf{Z}_{x_{0}}\}
.

7.7.
Example:
\SL_{n}
and unramified
\SU_{n}

Suppose
\mathbf{G}_{+}
is the
k
-group
\SL_{n}
and
\mathbf{G}_{-}
is the
k
-group unramified
\SU_{n}
. Suppose
p
does not divide
n
, and let
w_{\sigma}
be a Coxeter element in
\tilde{W}
. Note that the Coxeter class is the only
\sigma
-elliptic class in
\tilde{W}
.

From the discussion in §
7.6
, we can choose a
k
-torus
\mathbf{T}
in the
G_{\pm}
-orbit
\varphi_{\sigma}({{}^{\tilde{W}}\!}w_{\sigma})
and the elements of the set of
k
-stable classes in
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
are indexed by
\langle w_{\sigma}\rangle/\langle w_{\sigma}^{q-1}\rangle
, which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/(n,q-1)
.

Since
\mathbf{G}_{\pm}
is simply connected, from Remark
7.4.10
we have
\mathsf{T}_{F}\simeq\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
, which, in this case, may be identified with
\mu_{n}
, the center of
\mathbf{G}_{\pm}
. The action of
w_{\Fro}
on
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
is trivial and the action of
{\Fro}
on
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}
is given by
x\mapsto(x^{\pm 1})^{q}
. Thus,
(\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma})\sim_{w_{\Fro}\circ{\Fro}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/(n,q\mp 1)
.
We conclude that there are, up to
G_{\pm}^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy,
(n,q\mp 1)

k
-embeddings of
\mathbf{T}
into
\mathbf{G}
.

The set
\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}^{k}_{T}
breaks into
(n,q-1)

k
-stable classes. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
is a
k
-torus in one of these classes, then, up to
G_{\pm}^{{\Fro}}
-conjugacy, it can be embedded into
\mathbf{G}_{\pm}
in
(n,q\mp 1)
ways.

7.8.
Example:
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}

Suppose
p>2
. We adopt the notation of Example
3.6.2
.

As discussed in Example
3.6.2
there are two
G
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
, denoted
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
, corresponding to the two elliptic
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy classes
{C_{2}}
and
{-1}
. Since
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
is
k
-split, from Corollary
5.4.3
each of
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
contains tori that are defined over
k
.

A Coxeter element of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
has order
4
and the center of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{2}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{C_{2}}
decomposes into
(q-1,4)

k
-stable classes. If
\mathbf{T}
is a torus in one of these classes, then, up to
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy, it
k
-embeds into
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
in two ways. Since
\bar{W}_{T}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{8}
, the number of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{C_{2}}
is
(q-1,8)
.

The set
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into five
k
-stable orbits, corresponding to the five conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}^{-1}=\tilde{W}
. Note that we can take
w_{\Fro}
to be the trivial element in
\tilde{W}
. A conjugacy class
c
in
\tilde{W}
will be called
\alpha
-even if for some (hence any) element
w
in
c
we have that
w_{\alpha}
appears an even number of times in some (hence any) expression for
w
in terms of the simple reflections
w_{\alpha}
and
w_{\beta}
. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is indexed by an
\alpha
-even conjugacy class (of which there are three), then, up to
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has four
k
-embeddings into
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is not indexed by an
\alpha
-even conjugacy class (of which there are two), then, up to
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has two
k
-embeddings into
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
.

For some choice of
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
, the group
\tilde{C}_{2}:=N_{G_{x_{T},0}}(\mathbf{T})/T_{0}
has
32
elements and is isomorphic to the group, under matrix multiplication, of two-by-two matrices whose elements look like

\begin{bmatrix}a&0\\
0&b\end{bmatrix}\,\text{ or }\,\begin{bmatrix}0&c\\
d&0\end{bmatrix}

with
a,b,c,d\in\{1,i,-1,-i\}
and
{\Fro}(i)=\pm i
depending on whether or not
-1
is a square in
{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}
. One calculates that
if
4
does not divide
q-1
, then
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into six
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes.
If
4
does divide
q-1
, then
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into fourteen
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes; more specifically, in this case two of the stable classes break into two
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-orbits each, two break into three
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-orbits each, and one breaks into four
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}(k)
-orbits.

7.9.
Example:
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}

Suppose
p>2
. We adopt the notation of Example
7.8
.

There are two
G
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
, denoted
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
, corresponding to the two elliptic
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy classes
{C_{2}}
and
{-1}
. Since
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
is
k
-split, from Corollary
5.4.3
each of
\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
contains tori that are defined over
k
.

A Coxeter element of
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
has order
4
and the center of
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
is trivial. Therefore,
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{C_{2}}
decomposes into
(q-1,4)

k
-stable classes. If
\mathbf{T}
is a torus in one of these classes, then, up to
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy, it
k
-embeds into
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
in one way; hence, the number of
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{C_{2}}
is also
(q-1,4)
.

As in the case of
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{4}
, the set
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into five
k
-stable orbits indexed by the
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy classes in
\tilde{W}
. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is indexed by an
\alpha
-even conjugacy class, then, up to
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has two
k
-embeddings into
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
. If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is not indexed by an
\alpha
-even conjugacy class, then, up to
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has one
k
-embedding into
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}
.

For some choice of
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
, the group
N_{G_{x_{T},0}}(\mathbf{T})/T_{0}
has
16
elements and is isomorphic to the
\tilde{C}_{2}/\mu_{2}
. One calculates that
if
4
does not divide
q-1
, then
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into six
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes.
If
4
does divide
q-1
, then
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
breaks into ten
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-conjugacy classes; more specifically, in this case each stable classes breaks into two
{\mathrm{PSp}}_{4}(k)
-orbits each.

7.10.
Example:
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}

Suppose
p>3
. We adopt the notation of Example
3.6.3
. As discussed in Example
3.6.3
there are three
G
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
, denoted
\mathcal{O}_{G_{2}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
corresponding to the three elliptic
\tilde{W}
-conjugacy classes
G_{2}
,
A_{2}
, and
-1
.
Since
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
is
k
-split, from Corollary
5.4.3
each of
\mathcal{O}_{G_{2}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{-1}
contains tori that are defined over
k
.

A Coxeter element of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
has order
6
and the center of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
is trivial. Therefore,
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{G_{2}}
decomposes into
(q-1,6)

k
-stable classes. Since
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
has trivial center, if
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
is a torus in one of these classes, then, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy, it
k
-embeds into
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
in one way. This shows that the number of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy classes in
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{G_{2}}
is
(q-1,6)
; this can also be verified by calculating
\bar{W}_{T}
.

Suppose
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
. The group
\bar{W}_{T}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{6}
, and
\mathsf{T}_{F}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{3}
. One calculates that
(\bar{W}_{T})_{{\Fro}}=(\bar{W}_{T})_{{\sim_{{\Fro}}}}
is
\mu_{6}({\mathfrak{f}})
– that is, there are six
k
-stable classes in
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
when the cubic roots of unity belong to
{\mathfrak{f}}
and two otherwise. For half of the
k
-stable classes in
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
a torus
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
in the class will embed, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy, into
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
in three ways. For the other half of the classes a torus
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
in the class will embed, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy, into
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
in one way. Finally, there are twelve
G^{\Fro}
-conjugacy classes of tori in
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
when
3=\left|\mu_{3}({\mathfrak{f}})\right|
and three otherwise.

Alternatively, if
w_{\sigma}=w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}
, then
W_{T}\cong\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}\sigma}=\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}=\langle w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}\rangle
.
Up to left multiplication by an element of
\tilde{W}^{w_{\sigma}}
, from Corollary
7.4.5
we have
w_{\Fro}
is trivial if
q
is congruent to
1
modulo
6
and
w_{\Fro}
is
w_{\alpha}
if
q
is congruent to
-1
modulo
6
. (Note that
\mu_{3}({\mathfrak{f}})=1
if and only if
q
is congruent to
-1
mod
6
.) When
w_{\Fro}=1
, there are six
k
-stable classes in
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
and a torus
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
indexed by
(w_{\alpha}w_{\beta})^{j}\in\langle w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}\rangle
will, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy,
k
-embed into
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
in three ways when
j
is even and in one way when
j
is odd. When
w_{\Fro}
is
w_{\alpha}
, then there are two
K
-stable classes in
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
and a torus
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
indexed by
\bar{w}\in\langle w_{\alpha}w_{\beta}\rangle/\langle(w_{\alpha}w_{\beta})^{2}\rangle
will, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy,
k
-embed into
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
in three ways if
\bar{w}
is not trivial and in one way if
\bar{w}
is trivial.

If
\left|\mu_{3}({\mathfrak{f}})\right|=3
, then
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
breaks into twelve
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy classes. If
\left|\mu_{3}({\mathfrak{f}})\right|=1
, then
\mathcal{O}_{A_{2}}^{k}
breaks into three
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy classes.

For some choice of
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
, the group
\bar{W}_{T}
is isomorphic to the Weyl group of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
and
\mathsf{T}_{F}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{2}\times\mu_{2}
. Thus

\mathcal{O}^{k}_{-1}
decomposes into six
k
-stable classes – one for each
w_{\Fro}
-conjugacy class in
\tilde{W}^{-1}=\tilde{W}
.
If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is indexed by a
w_{\Fro}
-conjugacy class with one element then, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has four
k
-embeddings into
\mathbf{G}
.
If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is indexed by a
w_{\Fro}
-conjugacy class with three elements then, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has two
k
-embeddings into
\mathbf{G}
.
If
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
belongs to a
k
-stable class that is indexed by a
w_{\Fro}
-conjugacy class with two elements then, up to
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
has one
k
-embedding into
\mathbf{G}
.

The group
N_{G_{x_{T},0}}(\mathbf{T})/T_{0}
has
48
elements and is isomorphic to a Tit’s group of
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}
with generators
n_{\alpha}
and
n_{\beta}
of order four where
{\Fro}(n_{\alpha})=n_{\alpha}
and
{\Fro}(n_{\beta})=n_{\beta}^{q}
.
Independent of how
{\Fro}
acts,
\mathcal{O}_{-1}^{l}
breaks into ten
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy classes. The
k
-stable classes indexed by a
w_{\Fro}
-class with an odd number of elements each break into two
{\mathrm{G}_{2}}(k)
-conjugacy classes.

7.11.
Example: ramified
\SU_{3}

Suppose
p>3
.
We adopt the notation of Example
3.6.4
. As discussed in Example
3.6.4
there are two
G
-conjugacy classes of
K
-minisotropic maximal
K
-tori in ramified
\SU_{3}
,
denoted
\mathcal{O}_{c_{w_{\alpha}}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{c_{w_{0}}}
, corresponding to the twisted Coxeter element
w_{\alpha}\sigma
and the element
-1=w_{0}\sigma
. Since
c_{w_{\alpha}}=({\Fro}\circ N_{q})(c_{w_{\alpha}})
and
c_{w_{0}}=({\Fro}\circ N_{q})(c_{w_{0}})
, from Corollary
5.1.3
we conclude that each of
\mathcal{O}_{c_{w_{\alpha}}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{c_{w_{0}}}
contains tori that are defined over
k
.

Suppose
\mathbf{T}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{c_{w_{\alpha}}}
. Then
{W}_{T}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{3}
. Therefore, for the twisted Coxeter class, we have that
\mathcal{O}^{k}_{c_{w_{\alpha}}}
decomposes into
\left|(\mu_{3})_{{\Fro}}\right|=\left|\mu_{3}({\mathfrak{f}})\right|

k
-stable classes. Since
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\displaystyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\textstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.67998pt}{\resizebox{471860}{393216}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94443pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mathstrut$}}}}}}{}^{w_{\alpha}\sigma}\cong\bar{\mathsf{T}}_{F}
is trivial, for
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
in a given
k
